    "content": "You are a coding agent working on a bug-repair task. Use repository tools to inspect, edit, and run checks. The task environment contains a writable pinned repository. The task repository is the only mounted host directory.\n\nAvailable tools:\n- read_file: Read a UTF-8 file from the task repository. Arguments: path.\n- search_text: Search repository text, case-insensitively. Arguments: query.\n- apply_patch: Create a file with path/content, or replace one exact old_text match with new_text.\n- run_shell: Run a shell command from the task repository root. Arguments: command.",
    "role": "system"
  },
  {
    "content": "Task description:\n# Repair title-only retrieval relevance\n\nThe tutor's top-k retrieval can omit a useful section when the question matches\nthe section heading but the section body does not repeat those words. The\nproblem is visible in the selected passages and in the source text sent to the\ntutor model.\n\nMake the relevant section available when its heading matches the question, even\nwhen an unrelated section appears first and `k=1`. Keep body-text relevance and\nthe existing top-k, case-normalization, input-immutability, full-context,\nhistory, and mode behavior intact.\n\nThe task is limited to the retrieval and tutor-context behavior in\n`app/source.py` and `app/tutor.py`. A fake tutor response is supplied by the\noffline checks; no model service or network access is needed.\n\n\nRepository instructions (AGENTS.md):\n# AGENTS.md\n\nConventions for any coding assistant (GitHub Copilot in VS Code, Codespaces or\nthe CLI) working in this repository.\n\n## What this project is\nAn ML tutor chat for classmates: it answers questions on linear/softmax\nregression, weight decay, gradient descent, perceptrons, MLPs and\nbackpropagation, citing a section id like `[3.7.1]` for every fact. One\nStreamlit screen, nothing else: no login, no settings, no database, no user\naccounts. If a change adds a second screen, stop and ask.\n\n## Layout\n- `app/tutor.py` holds `answer(question, ...)`, the one job. The screen and the evals both call it.\n- `app/source.py` loads `source/sections.jsonl`, retrieves sections (BM25) and parses citations.\n- `app/llm.py` is the only file that talks to a model (NVIDIA, OpenAI-compatible). Keep tutor and judge on separate model families; IDs are configured in `.env.example`.\n- `prompts/tutor_vN.md` holds the tutor's prompts. Never edit an evaluated prompt: copy to the next version.\n- `source/` is the only text the tutor may teach from (CC BY-SA, see `source/ATTRIBUTION.md`).\n- `evals/` holds the golden set, code checks, the LLM judge and its rubric and calibration, results and the metric write-up.\n- `docs/` holds the iteration log and the report and slide outlines.\n\n## Rules\n- Synthetic or public text only. Never add real personal data to any file.\n- Never add text from Géron's book (or any copyrighted source) to the repo; this repo goes public.\n- Never commit `.env` or any API key.\n- Never change the expected sections or key facts in `evals/golden_set.jsonl`, or the\n  human labels in `evals/rubric/calibration.jsonl`, to make a score go up. Fix the app or the rubric instead.\n- Every evaluated change gets an entry in `docs/iteration_log.md`, including the ones we abandon.\n- Keep it small enough to demo in ninety seconds. Polish is not marked.\n- Run `python -m unittest` before committing; it needs no API key.\n\n## Skills\n`.github/skills/` holds the skills Copilot can load. `run-evals` is this\nproject's own procedure; the rest are Matt Pocock's engineering skills\n(MIT, see `.github/skills/LICENSE-mattpocock-skills`). Useful ones here:\n`tdd` for eval code, `grilling` to stress-test the idea, `code-review` before\na submission commit, `diagnosing-bugs` when the demo breaks.\n\n\nRepository file map:\n- .env.example\n- .gitignore\n- AGENTS.md\n- README.md\n- requirements.txt\n- app/__init__.py\n- app/llm.py\n- app/source.py\n- app/streamlit_app.py\n- app/tutor.py\n- tests/__init__.py\n- """The tutor's source text: load it, find passages, read citations back out.

Sections come from source/sections.jsonl (built by scripts/build_source.py).
Citations are section ids in square brackets, e.g. [3.7.1] or [12.4.1, 3.1].
"""

import json
import math
import re
from collections import Counter
from functools import lru_cache
from pathlib import Path

SECTIONS_FILE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent / "source" / "sections.jsonl"
CITATION = re.compile(r"\[((?:[0-9W]+(?:\.[0-9]+)*)(?:\s*,\s*[0-9W]+(?:\.[0-9]+)*)*)\]")
WORD = re.compile(r"[a-z0-9]+")
STOPWORDS = set("the a an of to and or in on is are be by for with what why how does do it its "
                "this that as at from can when which who use used".split())


@lru_cache(maxsize=1)
def load_sections() -> tuple[dict, ...]:
    lines = SECTIONS_FILE.read_text().splitlines()
    return tuple(json.loads(line) for line in lines if line.strip())


def extract_citations(text: str) -> list[str]:
    ids = []
    for group in CITATION.findall(text):
        ids += [part.strip() for part in group.split(",")]
    return ids


def passages_for(ids: list[str], sections) -> list[dict]:
    """Every chunk a citation covers: [3.1] covers 3.1, 3.1.1, 3.1.2, ... (not 3.10)."""
    return [s for s in sections if any(s["id"] == i or s["id"].startswith(i + ".") for i in ids)]


def _terms(text: str) -> list[str]:
    return [w for w in WORD.findall(text.lower()) if w not in STOPWORDS]


def retrieve(question: str, sections, k: int = 4) -> list[dict]:
    """BM25 over title + text. Small, dependency-free, and easy to explain in the report."""
    docs = [_terms(s["text"]) for s in sections]
    avg_len = sum(map(len, docs)) / len(docs)
    df = Counter(t for d in docs for t in set(d))
    n = len(docs)

    def score(doc: list[str]) -> float:
        tf = Counter(doc)
        total = 0.0
        for t in set(_terms(question)):
            if t not in tf:
                continue
            idf = math.log(1 + (n - df[t] + 0.5) / (df[t] + 0.5))
            total += idf * tf[t] * 2.2 / (tf[t] + 1.2 * (0.25 + 0.75 * len(doc) / avg_len))
        return total

    ranked = sorted(zip(sections, docs), key=lambda pair: score(pair[1]), reverse=True)
    return [s for s, _ in ranked[:k]]


def format_context(sections) -> str:
    return "\n\n".join(
        f'<section id="{s["id"]}" title="{s["title"]}" url="{s["url"]}">\n{s["text"]}\n</section>'
        for s in sections
    )

"""The one job: answer a classmate's question from the source, with citations.

Two ways to give the model the source, compared in the evals:
  retrieval="topk"  only the k sections BM25 ranks highest for the question (default)
  retrieval="full"  every section in the prompt. Abandoned on 2026-10-07: the
                    source grew to ~1.24M characters, past the model's context
                    window (see docs/iteration_log.md).
"""

from app.source import format_context, load_sections, retrieve

DEFAULT_PROMPT = "prompts/tutor_v1.md"
MODES = ("explain", "quiz")


def answer(question: str, chat=None, sections=None, prompt: str | None = None,
           history: list[dict] | None = None, mode: str = "explain",
           retrieval: str = "topk", k: int = 4) -> str:
    if chat is None:
        from app.llm import tutor_chat as chat
    if prompt is None:
        from app.llm import load_prompt
        prompt = load_prompt(DEFAULT_PROMPT)
    if sections is None:
        sections = load_sections()
    if mode not in MODES:
        raise ValueError(f"mode must be one of {MODES}")

    if retrieval == "full":
        context = sections
    elif retrieval == "topk":
        context = retrieve(question, sections, k=k)
    else:
        raise ValueError("retrieval must be 'full' or 'topk'")

    system = f"{prompt}\n\nMode: {mode}\n\n<source>\n{format_context(context)}\n</source>"
    messages = [*(history or []), {"role": "user", "content": question}]
    return chat(system, messages)

import unittest

from app.source import extract_citations, passages_for, retrieve

SECTIONS = [
    {"id": "3.1", "title": "Linear Regression", "url": "u", "text": "Regression predicts a number."},
    {"id": "3.1.1", "title": "Basics", "url": "u", "text": "Squared loss and the analytic solution."},
    {"id": "3.7.1", "title": "Norms and Weight Decay", "url": "u",
     "text": "Weight decay adds an L2 penalty on the weights to the loss."},
    {"id": "3.10", "title": "Unrelated", "url": "u", "text": "Something else entirely."},
    {"id": "12.4.1", "title": "Stochastic Gradient Updates", "url": "u",
     "text": "Stochastic gradient descent uses one example per update."},
]


class ExtractCitationsTest(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_finds_bracketed_section_ids_including_lists(self):
        text = "Weight decay shrinks weights [3.7.1]. SGD is noisy [12.4.1, 3.1]. Not [this]."
        self.assertEqual(extract_citations(text), ["3.7.1", "12.4.1", "3.1"])


class PassagesForTest(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_a_section_citation_covers_its_subsections_but_not_lookalikes(self):
        ids = [s["id"] for s in passages_for(["3.1"], SECTIONS)]
        self.assertEqual(ids, ["3.1", "3.1.1"])


class RetrieveTest(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_ranks_the_section_that_shares_the_question_terms_first(self):
        top = retrieve("what does weight decay do to the weights?", SECTIONS, k=2)
        self.assertEqual(top[0]["id"], "3.7.1")
        self.assertEqual(len(top), 2)

import unittest

from app.tutor import answer

SECTIONS = (
    {"id": "3.7.1", "title": "Norms and Weight Decay", "url": "u",
     "text": "Weight decay adds an L2 penalty on the weights to the loss."},
    {"id": "12.4.1", "title": "Stochastic Gradient Updates", "url": "u",
     "text": "Stochastic gradient descent uses one example per update."},
)


class FakeChat:
    def __init__(self, reply="ok [3.7.1]"):
        self.reply, self.calls = reply, []

    def __call__(self, system, messages):
        self.calls.append((system, messages))
        return self.reply


class AnswerTest(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_full_context_puts_every_section_in_the_system_prompt(self):
        chat = FakeChat()
        answer("What is weight decay?", chat=chat, sections=SECTIONS, prompt="RULES",
               retrieval="full")
        system, messages = chat.calls[0]
        self.assertIn("RULES", system)
        self.assertIn('id="3.7.1"', system)
        self.assertIn('id="12.4.1"', system)
        self.assertEqual(messages[-1], {"role": "user", "content": "What is weight decay?"})

    def test_topk_puts_only_the_retrieved_sections_in_the_prompt(self):
        chat = FakeChat()
        answer("What is weight decay?", chat=chat, sections=SECTIONS, prompt="RULES",
               retrieval="topk", k=1)
        system, _ = chat.calls[0]
        self.assertIn('id="3.7.1"', system)
        self.assertNotIn('id="12.4.1"', system)

    def test_default_sends_only_retrieved_sections_so_a_large_source_fits(self):
        chat = FakeChat()
        answer("What is weight decay?", chat=chat, sections=SECTIONS, prompt="RULES", k=1)
        system, _ = chat.calls[0]
        self.assertIn('id="3.7.1"', system)
        self.assertNotIn('id="12.4.1"', system)

    def test_history_and_mode_reach_the_model(self):
        chat = FakeChat()
        history = [{"role": "user", "content": "hi"}, {"role": "assistant", "content": "hello"}]
        answer("quiz me", chat=chat, sections=SECTIONS, prompt="RULES", history=history,
               mode="quiz")
        system, messages = chat.calls[0]
        self.assertIn("Mode: quiz", system)
        self.assertEqual(messages[:2], history)

Small, dependency-free, and easy to explain in the report.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    docs = [_terms(s[\\\"text\\\"]) for s in sections]\\n    avg_len = sum(map(len, docs)) / len(docs)\\n    df = Counter(t for d in docs for t in set(d))\\n    n = len(docs)\\n\\n    def score(doc: list[str]) -> float:\\n        tf = Counter(doc)\\n        total = 0.0\\n        for t in set(_terms(question)):\\n            if t not in tf:\\n                continue\\n            idf = math.log(1 + (n - df[t] + 0.5) / (df[t] + 0.5))\\n            total += idf * tf[t] * 2.2 / (tf[t] + 1.2 * (0.25 + 0.75 * len(doc) / avg_len))\\n        return total\\n\\n    ranked = sorted(zip(sections, docs), key=lambda pair: score(pair[1]), reverse=True)\\n    return [s for s, _ in ranked[:k]]\\n\\n\\ndef format_context(sections) -> str:\\n    return \\\"\\\\n\\\\n\\\".join(\\n        f'<section id=\\\"{s[\\\"id\\\"]}\\\" title=\\\"{s[\\\"title\\\"]}\\\" url=\\\"{s[\\\"url\\\"]}\\\">\\\\n{s[\\\"text\\\"]}\\\\n</section>'\\n        for s in sections\\n    )\\n\", \"retryable\": false, \"status\": \"ok\", \"truncated\": false}",

# Iteration log

Every attempt, including the ones we abandon (10% of the mark). One entry per
change: what we tried, how we measured it, what happened, and why we kept or
dropped it. Link the results file for each run.

## Planned first comparison: whole source vs retrieved sections
- **v1-full:** `python -m evals.run_evals --retrieval full` (all ~32k tokens of source in the prompt)
- **v1-topk:** `python -m evals.run_evals --retrieval topk --k 4` (BM25, 4 best subsections)
- Compare grounded answer rate, `correct`, cost and latency. Keep one, and log
  why the other was abandoned.

## Tutor model · 2026-10-06
- **Tried:** DeepSeek V4 Pro, then DeepSeek V4.1 Flash, then gpt-oss-20b (all on NVIDIA). Judge stays Kimi K3.
- **Measured:** one-word prompt ("Capital of France?"), wall time: V4 Pro 410 Gone (end of life 2026-09-14);
  V4.1 Flash 174 s (a full cited answer took 178 s, so the wait is NVIDIA's queue, not prompt size);
  gpt-oss-20b 1.0 s.
- **Outcome:** gpt-oss-20b kept, since a three-minute reply can't be demoed. Not yet scored on the golden set;
  the v1 runs below are the first to use it.

## Judge model · 2026-10-07
- **Tried:** Kimi K3 was reported to take about three minutes to first token. Poolside Laguna XS 2.1 was rejected as too small before a model call. Gemma 4 31B IT was tried next, but its calibration request waited over five minutes for response headers. Switched the judge to Meta Muse Glimmer 30B, a separate family from the gpt-oss-20b tutor. The Muse call uses temperature 0.95, top-p 1, low reasoning effort, and seed 0, following NVIDIA's API guidance for this reasoning model.
- **Measured:** the Kimi smoke run is recorded in `evals/results/20261007-190049-tutor_v1-full.jsonl`; all 3 cases ended with `APIConnectionError`. NVIDIA's metadata route returned HTTP 200 in 0.15 s; a gpt-oss-20b control call returned at the API level in 0.8 s, though its text was empty at an eight-token cap. The first Muse prompt returned valid JSON within a 15-second timeout. Calibration matched both rows, but `calibration.jsonl` identifies them as hand-written format examples, so this is not meaningful calibration against real tutor outputs.
- **Outcome:** Muse Glimmer is configured and the top-k eval below completed. Judge reliability remains provisional: 2/24 full-run cases failed judge parsing, and the calibration fixture has only two format examples.

## v1-full smoke · 2026-10-07
- **Tried:** Send the full source to the tutor for a three-case smoke run.
- **Measured:** `evals/results/20261007-192800-tutor_v1-full.jsonl`; all 3 requests failed with HTTP 400 because the expanded source exceeded the model's context limit (`max_tokens` calculated below zero).
- **Outcome:** Full-source retrieval is not viable with the current 335-section, 1.24-million-character source. Switched to top-4 retrieval for the scored run.

## v1-topk4 · 2026-10-07
- **Tried:** `python -m evals.run_evals --prompt prompts/tutor_v1.md --retrieval topk --k 4`.
- **Measured:** smoke results are in `evals/results/20261007-192951-tutor_v1-topk4.jsonl` (3 cases, 0 errors, grounded answer rate 33.3%). The full results are in `evals/results/20261007-194045-tutor_v1-topk4.jsonl` (24 cases, 2 judge-parse errors, 8 grounded answers; grounded answer rate 33.3%, citation check 59.1%, check-question check 95.5%, judge supported 36.4%, judge correct 77.3%). The full-run rates exclude the two parse errors except grounded answer rate, whose denominator is all 24 cases.
- **Outcome:** Top-4 retrieval completed the full set; the prompt remains unchanged. The measured grounded answer rate is low, and the two judge-parse errors plus placeholder calibration limit confidence in the score. Failure patterns from all 24 answers are recorded in `evals/failure_taxonomy.md`.

## Judge model · 2026-10-09
- **Tried:** Replaced Muse Glimmer 30B with NVIDIA Nemotron 3 Super 120B (`nvidia/nemotron-3-super-120b-a12b`) as judge, still a separate family from the gpt-oss-20b tutor. Muse was abandoned because 2/24 full-run cases failed judge parsing, and NVIDIA's guidance has it sampled at temperature 0.95, so verdicts are not repeatable. Nemotron uses the default temperature-0 path. In a one-line JSON probe on 2026-10-07 it answered in 1.3 s; Kimi K3 took 48 s.
- **Measured:** `python -m evals.run_evals --prompt prompts/tutor_v1.md --retrieval topk --k 4`. Smoke: `evals/results/20261009-142243-tutor_v1-topk4.jsonl` (3 cases, 0 errors, grounded 33.3%). Full: `evals/results/20261009-143024-tutor_v1-topk4.jsonl` (24 cases, 0 parse errors, 6 grounded answers; grounded answer rate 25.0%, citation check 41.7%, check-question check 87.5%, judge supported 25.0%, judge correct 83.3%), 7.5 min wall time. Muse run for comparison: grounded 33.3%, citation 59.1%, supported 36.4%, correct 77.3%, 2 parse errors.
- **Outcome:** Nemotron kept as judge: no parse errors and deterministic decoding. The runs are not a judge-only comparison, because the tutor answers were regenerated: the citation check is code, not the judge, and it fell from 59.1% to 41.7%, so tutor variance explains part of the lower grounded rate. Calibration still uses only the two hand-written format examples in `evals/rubric/calibration.jsonl`, so the judge is not really calibrated yet.

Small, dependency-free, and easy to explain in the report.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    docs = [_terms(s[\\\"text\\\"]) for s in sections]\\n    avg_len = sum(map(len, docs)) / len(docs)\\n    df = Counter(t for d in docs for t in set(d))\\n    n = len(docs)\\n\\n    def score(doc: list[str]) -> float:\\n        tf = Counter(doc)\\n        total = 0.0\\n        for t in set(_terms(question)):\\n            if t not in tf:\\n                continue\\n            idf = math.log(1 + (n - df[t] + 0.5) / (df[t] + 0.5))\\n            total += idf * tf[t] * 2.2 / (tf[t] + 1.2 * (0.25 + 0.75 * len(doc) / avg_len))\\n        return total\\n\\n    ranked = sorted(zip(sections, docs), key=lambda pair: score(pair[1]), reverse=True)\\n    return [s for s, _ in ranked[:k]]\\n\\n\\ndef format_context(sections) -> str:\\n    return \\\"\\\\n\\\\n\\\".join(\\n        f'<section id=\\\"{s[\\\"id\\\"]}\\\" title=\\\"{s[\\\"title\\\"]}\\\" url=\\\"{s[\\\"url\\\"]}\\\">\\\\n{s[\\\"text\\\"]}\\\\n</section>'\\n        for s in sections\\n    )\\n\", \"retryable\": false, \"status\": \"ok\", \"truncated\": false}",

A fake tutor response is supplied by the\noffline checks; no model service or network access is needed.\n\n\nRepository instructions (AGENTS.md):\n# AGENTS.md\n\nConventions for any coding assistant (GitHub Copilot in VS Code, Codespaces or\nthe CLI) working in this repository.\n\n## What this project is\nAn ML tutor chat for classmates: it answers questions on linear/softmax\nregression, weight decay, gradient descent, perceptrons, MLPs and\nbackpropagation, citing a section id like `[3.7.1]` for every fact. One\nStreamlit screen, nothing else: no login, no settings, no database, no user\naccounts. If a change adds a second screen, stop and ask.\n\n## Layout\n- `app/tutor.py` holds `answer(question, ...)`, the one job. The screen and the evals both call it.\n- `app/source.py` loads `source/sections.jsonl`, retrieves sections (BM25) and parses citations.\n- `app/llm.py` is the only file that talks to a model (NVIDIA, OpenAI-compatible). Keep tutor and judge on separate model families; IDs are configured in `.env.example`.\n- `prompts/tutor_vN.md` holds the tutor's prompts. Never edit an evaluated prompt: copy to the next version.\n- `source/` is the only text the tutor may teach from (CC BY-SA, see `source/ATTRIBUTION.md`).\n- `evals/` holds the golden set, code checks, the LLM judge and its rubric and calibration, results and the metric write-up.\n- `docs/` holds the iteration log and the report and slide outlines.\n\n## Rules\n- Synthetic or public text only. Never add real personal data to any file.\n- Never add text from Géron's book (or any copyrighted source) to the repo; this repo goes public.\n- Never commit `.env` or any API key.\n- Never change the expected sections or key facts in `evals/golden_set.jsonl`, or the\n  human labels in `evals/rubric/calibration.jsonl`, to make a score go up. Fix the app or the rubric instead.\n- Every evaluated change gets an entry in `docs/iteration_log.md`, including the ones we abandon.\n- Keep it small enough to demo in ninety seconds. Polish is not marked.\n- Run `python -m unittest` before committing; it needs no API key.\n\n## Skills\n`.github/skills/` holds the skills Copilot can load. `run-evals` is this\nproject's own procedure; the rest are Matt Pocock's engineering skills\n(MIT, see `.github/skills/LICENSE-mattpocock-skills`). Useful ones here:\n`tdd` for eval code, `grilling` to stress-test the idea, `code-review` before\na submission commit, `diagnosing-bugs` when the demo breaks.\n\n\nRepository file map:\n- (not 3.10).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    return [s for s in sections if any(s[\\\"id\\\"] == i or s[\\\"id\\\"].startswith(i + \\\".\\\") for i in ids)]\\n\\n\\ndef _terms(text: str) -> list[str]:\\n    return [w for w in WORD.findall(text.lower()) if w not in STOPWORDS]\\n\\n\\ndef retrieve(question: str, sections, k: int = 4) -> list[dict]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"BM25 over title + text. Small, dependency-free, and easy to explain in the report.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    docs = [_terms(s[\\\"text\\\"]) for s in sections]\\n    avg_len = sum(map(len, docs)) / len(docs)\\n    df = Counter(t for d in docs for t in set(d))\\n    n = len(docs)\\n\\n    def score(doc: list[str]) -> float:\\n        tf = Counter(doc)\\n        total = 0.0\\n        for t in set(_terms(question)):\\n            if t not in tf:\\n                continue\\n            idf = math.log(1 + (n - df[t] + 0.5) / (df[t] + 0.5))\\n            total += idf * tf[t] * 2.2 / (tf[t] + 1.2 * (0.25 + 0.75 * len(doc) / avg_len))\\n        return total\\n\\n    ranked = sorted(zip(sections, docs), key=lambda pair: score(pair[1]), reverse=True)\\n    return [s for s, _ in ranked[:k]]\\n\\n\\ndef format_context(sections) -> str:\\n    return \\\"\\\\n\\\\n\\\".join(\\n        f'<section id=\\\"{s[\\\"id\\\"]}\\\" title=\\\"{s[\\\"title\\\"]}\\\" url=\\\"{s[\\\"url\\\"]}\\\">\\\\n{s[\\\"text\\\"]}\\\\n</section>'\\n        for s in sections\\n    )\\n\", \"retryable\": false, \"status\": \"ok\", \"truncated\": false}",

(not 3.10).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    return [s for s in sections if any(s[\\\"id\\\"] == i or s[\\\"id\\\"].startswith(i + \\\".\\\") for i in ids)]\\n\\n\\ndef _terms(text: str) -> list[str]:\\n    return [w for w in WORD.findall(text.lower()) if w not in STOPWORDS]\\n\\n\\ndef retrieve(question: str, sections, k: int = 4) -> list[dict]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"BM25 over title + text. Small, dependency-free, and easy to explain in the report.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    docs = [_terms(s[\\\"text\\\"]) for s in sections]\\n    avg_len = sum(map(len, docs)) / len(docs)\\n    df = Counter(t for d in docs for t in set(d))\\n    n = len(docs)\\n\\n    def score(doc: list[str]) -> float:\\n        tf = Counter(doc)\\n        total = 0.0\\n        for t in set(_terms(question)):\\n            if t not in tf:\\n                continue\\n            idf = math.log(1 + (n - df[t] + 0.5) / (df[t] + 0.5))\\n            total += idf * tf[t] * 2.2 / (tf[t] + 1.2 * (0.25 + 0.75 * len(doc) / avg_len))\\n        return total\\n\\n    ranked = sorted(zip(sections, docs), key=lambda pair: score(pair[1]), reverse=True)\\n    return [s for s, _ in ranked[:k]]\\n\\n\\ndef format_context(sections) -> str:\\n    return \\\"\\\\n\\\\n\\\".join(\\n        f'<section id=\\\"{s[\\\"id\\\"]}\\\" title=\\\"{s[\\\"title\\\"]}\\\" url=\\\"{s[\\\"url\\\"]}\\\">\\\\n{s[\\\"text\\\"]}\\\\n</section>'\\n        for s in sections\\n    )\\n\", \"retryable\": false, \"status\": \"ok\", \"truncated\": false}",

(not 3.10).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    return [s for s in sections if any(s[\\\"id\\\"] == i or s[\\\"id\\\"].startswith(i + \\\".\\\") for i in ids)]\\n\\n\\ndef _terms(text: str) -> list[str]:\\n    return [w for w in WORD.findall(text.lower()) if w not in STOPWORDS]\\n\\n\\ndef retrieve(question: str, sections, k: int = 4) -> list[dict]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"BM25 over title + text. Small, dependency-free, and easy to explain in the report.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    docs = [_terms(s[\\\"text\\\"]) for s in sections]\\n    avg_len = sum(map(len, docs)) / len(docs)\\n    df = Counter(t for d in docs for t in set(d))\\n    n = len(docs)\\n\\n    def score(doc: list[str]) -> float:\\n        tf = Counter(doc)\\n        total = 0.0\\n        for t in set(_terms(question)):\\n            if t not in tf:\\n                continue\\n            idf = math.log(1 + (n - df[t] + 0.5) / (df[t] + 0.5))\\n            total += idf * tf[t] * 2.2 / (tf[t] + 1.2 * (0.25 + 0.75 * len(doc) / avg_len))\\n        return total\\n\\n    ranked = sorted(zip(sections, docs), key=lambda pair: score(pair[1]), reverse=True)\\n    return [s for s, _ in ranked[:k]]\\n\\n\\ndef format_context(sections) -> str:\\n    return \\\"\\\\n\\\\n\\\".join(\\n        f'<section id=\\\"{s[\\\"id\\\"]}\\\" title=\\\"{s[\\\"title\\\"]}\\\" url=\\\"{s[\\\"url\\\"]}\\\">\\\\n{s[\\\"text\\\"]}\\\\n</section>'\\n        for s in sections\\n    )\\n\", \"retryable\": false, \"status\": \"ok\", \"truncated\": false}",

(not 3.10).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    return [s for s in sections if any(s[\\\"id\\\"] == i or s[\\\"id\\\"].startswith(i + \\\".\\\") for i in ids)]\\n\\n\\ndef _terms(text: str) -> list[str]:\\n    return [w for w in WORD.findall(text.lower()) if w not in STOPWORDS]\\n\\n\\ndef retrieve(question: str, sections, k: int = 4) -> list[dict]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"BM25 over title + text. Small, dependency-free, and easy to explain in the report.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    docs = [_terms(s[\\\"text\\\"]) for s in sections]\\n    avg_len = sum(map(len, docs)) / len(docs)\\n    df = Counter(t for d in docs for t in set(d))\\n    n = len(docs)\\n\\n    def score(doc: list[str]) -> float:\\n        tf = Counter(doc)\\n        total = 0.0\\n        for t in set(_terms(question)):\\n            if t not in tf:\\n                continue\\n            idf = math.log(1 + (n - df[t] + 0.5) / (df[t] + 0.5))\\n            total += idf * tf[t] * 2.2 / (tf[t] + 1.2 * (0.25 + 0.75 * len(doc) / avg_len))\\n        return total\\n\\n    ranked = sorted(zip(sections, docs), key=lambda pair: score(pair[1]), reverse=True)\\n    return [s for s, _ in ranked[:k]]\\n\\n\\ndef format_context(sections) -> str:\\n    return \\\"\\\\n\\\\n\\\".join(\\n        f'<section id=\\\"{s[\\\"id\\\"]}\\\" title=\\\"{s[\\\"title\\\"]}\\\" url=\\\"{s[\\\"url\\\"]}\\\">\\\\n{s[\\\"text\\\"]}\\\\n</section>'\\n        for s in sections\\n    )\\n\", \"retryable\": false, \"status\": \"ok\", \"truncated\": false}",

(not 3.10).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    return [s for s in sections if any(s[\\\"id\\\"] == i or s[\\\"id\\\"].startswith(i + \\\".\\\") for i in ids)]\\n\\n\\ndef _terms(text: str) -> list[str]:\\n    return [w for w in WORD.findall(text.lower()) if w not in STOPWORDS]\\n\\n\\ndef retrieve(question: str, sections, k: int = 4) -> list[dict]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"BM25 over title + text. Small, dependency-free, and easy to explain in the report.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    docs = [_terms(s[\\\"text\\\"]) for s in sections]\\n    avg_len = sum(map(len, docs)) / len(docs)\\n    df = Counter(t for d in docs for t in set(d))\\n    n = len(docs)\\n\\n    def score(doc: list[str]) -> float:\\n        tf = Counter(doc)\\n        total = 0.0\\n        for t in set(_terms(question)):\\n            if t not in tf:\\n                continue\\n            idf = math.log(1 + (n - df[t] + 0.5) / (df[t] + 0.5))\\n            total += idf * tf[t] * 2.2 / (tf[t] + 1.2 * (0.25 + 0.75 * len(doc) / avg_len))\\n        return total\\n\\n    ranked = sorted(zip(sections, docs), key=lambda pair: score(pair[1]), reverse=True)\\n    return [s for s, _ in ranked[:k]]\\n\\n\\ndef format_context(sections) -> str:\\n    return \\\"\\\\n\\\\n\\\".join(\\n        f'<section id=\\\"{s[\\\"id\\\"]}\\\" title=\\\"{s[\\\"title\\\"]}\\\" url=\\\"{s[\\\"url\\\"]}\\\">\\\\n{s[\\\"text\\\"]}\\\\n</section>'\\n        for s in sections\\n    )\\n\", \"retryable\": false, \"status\": \"ok\", \"truncated\": false}",
